--- START FILE: tools/agent_bus.py ---
import time
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional
from tools.dashboard import dashboard

class AgentBlackboard:
    """
    Structured Hub-and-Spoke communication ledger.
    Subagents publish typed artifacts here, and peer agents read them
    without polluting context windows with raw conversational back-and-forth.
    """

    def __init__(self):
        self._artifacts: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []

    def publish(self, sender: str, artifact_type: str, payload: Any, metadata: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Publishes a typed artifact to the board.
        Types: PLAN, CODE_PATCH, SECURITY_AUDIT, REVIEW_DECISION
        """
        entry = {
            "id": len(self._artifacts) + 1,
            "sender": sender,
            "type": artifact_type,
            "payload": payload,
            "metadata": metadata or {},
            "timestamp": time.time(),
        }
        self._artifacts.append(entry)

        dashboard.emit("LOG_STREAM", {
            "message": f"[Blackboard] {sender} published {artifact_type} (#{entry['id']})"
        })
        return entry

    def get_latest(self, artifact_type: str) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
        for entry in reversed(self._artifacts):
            if entry["type"] == artifact_type:
                return entry
        return None

    def get_all(self, artifact_type: Optional[str] = None) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
        if artifact_type:
            return [e for e in self._artifacts if e["type"] == artifact_type]
        return list(self._artifacts)

    def clear(self) -> None:
        self._artifacts.clear()

# Global blackboard instance for current run
blackboard = AgentBlackboard()

--- END FILE: tools/agent_bus.py ---

--- START FILE: tools/agent_memory.py ---
import json
import re
import sqlite3
import time
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, Any, List, Optional

WORKSPACE_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
DATA_DIR = WORKSPACE_ROOT / "data"
DATA_DIR.mkdir(exist_ok=True)

class UserProfileMemory:
    """
    Tracks persistent user traits, coding habits, and preferred styles across sessions.
    """

    def __init__(self, storage_path: Optional[Path] = None):
        self.path = storage_path or (DATA_DIR / "user_profile.json")
        self.profile = self._load()

    def _load(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        if self.path.exists():
            try:
                with open(self.path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                    return json.load(f)
            except Exception:
                pass
        return {
            "user_name": "Developer",
            "preferred_languages": ["python"],
            "preferred_style": "minimal, modular, well-tested",
            "target_device": "mobile_termux",
            "custom_instructions": [],
            "interaction_count": 0
        }

    def save(self) -> None:
        try:
            with open(self.path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                json.dump(self.profile, f, indent=2)
        except Exception:
            pass

    def update_preference(self, key: str, value: Any) -> None:
        self.profile[key] = value
        self.save()

    def add_instruction(self, instruction: str) -> None:
        if instruction not in self.profile["custom_instructions"]:
            self.profile["custom_instructions"].append(instruction)
            self.save()

    def get_system_prompt_context(self) -> str:
        instructions = "; ".join(self.profile.get("custom_instructions", [])) or "None specified"
        return (
            f"\n[User Memory Context]\n"
            f"- User Name/Handle: {self.profile.get('user_name')}\n"
            f"- Preferred Languages: {', '.join(self.profile.get('preferred_languages', []))}\n"
            f"- Preferred Code Style: {self.profile.get('preferred_style')}\n"
            f"- Device Constraints: {self.profile.get('target_device')}\n"
            f"- Custom User Directives: {instructions}\n"
        )


class ConversationMemory:
    """
    Stores full chat history and uses SQLite FTS5 for recalling past discussions.
    """

    def __init__(self, db_path: Optional[Path] = None):
        self.db_path = db_path or (DATA_DIR / "conversation_history.db")
        self._init_db()

    def _init_db(self) -> None:
        with sqlite3.connect(self.db_path) as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()
            cursor.execute("""
                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS messages (
                    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
                    role TEXT,
                    content TEXT,
                    timestamp REAL
                )
            """)
            cursor.execute("""
                CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS messages_fts USING fts5(
                    content,
                    content='messages',
                    content_rowid='id'
                )
            """)
            conn.commit()

    def log_message(self, role: str, content: str) -> None:
        """Saves a single conversation turn (user or assistant)."""
        if not content.strip():
            return
        with sqlite3.connect(self.db_path) as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()
            cursor.execute(
                "INSERT INTO messages (role, content, timestamp) VALUES (?, ?, ?)",
                (role, content, time.time())
            )
            row_id = cursor.lastrowid
            cursor.execute(
                "INSERT INTO messages_fts (rowid, content) VALUES (?, ?)",
                (row_id, content)
            )
            conn.commit()

    def get_recent_history(self, limit: int = 6) -> List[Dict[str, str]]:
        """Returns the most recent N turns for active context."""
        with sqlite3.connect(self.db_path) as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()
            cursor.execute(
                "SELECT role, content FROM messages ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT ?",
                (limit,)
            )
            rows = cursor.fetchall()
            return [{"role": r[0], "content": r[1]} for r in reversed(rows)]

    def search_past_topics(self, query: str, limit: int = 3) -> List[str]:
        """Recalls relevant past user conversations based on keywords."""
        clean_words = re.findall(r"\b[A-Za-z0-9_]{3,}\b", query)
        if not clean_words:
            return []
        fts_query = " OR ".join(clean_words[:8])

        results = []
        try:
            with sqlite3.connect(self.db_path) as conn:
                cursor = conn.cursor()
                cursor.execute("""
                    SELECT m.role, m.content
                    FROM messages_fts f
                    JOIN messages m ON f.rowid = m.id
                    WHERE messages_fts MATCH ?
                    ORDER BY rank
                    LIMIT ?
                """, (fts_query, limit))
                for row in cursor.fetchall():
                    results.append(f"{row[0].capitalize()}: {row[1]}")
        except Exception:
            pass
        return results


class EpisodicExecutionMemory:
    """
    Stores code execution error traces and corresponding successful patches.
    """

    def __init__(self, db_path: Optional[Path] = None):
        self.db_path = db_path or (DATA_DIR / "episodic_memory.db")
        self._init_db()

    def _init_db(self) -> None:
        with sqlite3.connect(self.db_path) as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()
            cursor.execute("""
                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS episodes (
                    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
                    repo_name TEXT,
                    error_signature TEXT,
                    resolution_patch TEXT,
                    explanation TEXT,
                    timestamp REAL
                )
            """)
            cursor.execute("""
                CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS episodes_fts USING fts5(
                    error_signature,
                    explanation,
                    content='episodes',
                    content_rowid='id'
                )
            """)
            conn.commit()

    def record_resolution(self, repo_name: str, error_signature: str, resolution_patch: str, explanation: str) -> None:
        if not error_signature.strip():
            return
        with sqlite3.connect(self.db_path) as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()
            cursor.execute(
                "INSERT INTO episodes (repo_name, error_signature, resolution_patch, explanation, timestamp) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)",
                (repo_name, error_signature, resolution_patch, explanation, time.time())
            )
            row_id = cursor.lastrowid
            cursor.execute(
                "INSERT INTO episodes_fts (rowid, error_signature, explanation) VALUES (?, ?, ?)",
                (row_id, error_signature, explanation)
            )
            conn.commit()

    def recall_similar_fix(self, current_error: str, limit: int = 2) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
        if not current_error.strip():
            return []

        clean_words = re.findall(r"\b[A-Za-z0-9_]{3,}\b", current_error)
        if not clean_words:
            return []
        fts_query = " OR ".join(clean_words[:12])

        results = []
        try:
            with sqlite3.connect(self.db_path) as conn:
                cursor = conn.cursor()
                cursor.execute("""
                    SELECT e.repo_name, e.error_signature, e.resolution_patch, e.explanation
                    FROM episodes_fts f
                    JOIN episodes e ON f.rowid = e.id
                    WHERE episodes_fts MATCH ?
                    ORDER BY rank
                    LIMIT ?
                """, (fts_query, limit))
                for row in cursor.fetchall():
                    results.append({
                        "repo_name": row[0],
                        "error_signature": row[1],
                        "resolution_patch": row[2],
                        "explanation": row[3],
                    })
        except Exception:
            pass
        return results

--- START FILE: tools/dashboard.py ---
import os
import sys
import json
import html
import threading
import http.server
import socketserver
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, Any, List, Optional
from datetime import datetime

PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
if str(PROJECT_ROOT) not in sys.path:
    sys.path.insert(0, str(PROJECT_ROOT))

from tools.system_info import get_system_capabilities as get_system_info
from llm.router import HardwareSpecDetector, ModelRouter

OUTCOME_COLORS = {
    "pr_created": "#22c55e",
    "pushed_no_pr": "#3b82f6",
    "dry_run_complete": "#a855f7",
    "aborted": "#eab308",
    "failed": "#ef4444",
    "in_progress": "#6b7280",
}

def _read_history(logs_dir: Path) -> list:
    history_file = logs_dir / "history.jsonl"
    if not history_file.exists():
        return []
    rows = []
    with open(history_file) as f:
        for line in f:
            line = line.strip()
            if line:
                rows.append(json.loads(line))
    return list(reversed(rows))

def _row_html(row: dict) -> str:
    outcome = row.get("outcome", "unknown")
    color = OUTCOME_COLORS.get(outcome, "#6b7280")
    repo = html.escape(row.get("repository") or "—")
    issue = row.get("issue_number")
    issue_str = f"#{issue}" if issue else "—"
    dry = " (dry-run)" if row.get("dry_run") else ""
    started = row.get("started_at", "")
    prompt_tok = row.get("total_prompt_tokens", 0)
    completion_tok = row.get("total_completion_tokens", 0)
    calls = row.get("model_calls", 0)
    return f"""<tr>
      <td>{html.escape(started)}</td>
      <td>{repo}</td>
      <td>{issue_str}</td>
      <td><span style="background:{color}; padding: 2px 6px; border-radius: 4px;">{html.escape(outcome)}{dry}</span></td>
      <td>{calls}</td>
      <td>{prompt_tok} in / {completion_tok} out</td>
    </tr>"""

def generate_dashboard(logs_dir: Path, output_path: Path) -> Path:
    rows = _read_history(logs_dir)
    total_runs = len(rows)
    total_prompt = sum(r.get("total_prompt_tokens", 0) for r in rows)
    total_completion = sum(r.get("total_completion_tokens", 0) for r in rows)
    rows_html = "".join(_row_html(r) for r in rows) or '<tr><td colspan="6">No runs yet.</td></tr>'
    
    page = f"""<!DOCTYPE html>
<html><head><meta charset="UTF-8"><title>OSS-Agent Runs</title></head>
<body style="background:#0d1117; color:#f0f6fc; font-family:sans-serif; padding:2rem;">
  <h2>Run History ({total_runs} runs, {total_prompt + total_completion} tokens)</h2>
  <table border="1" cellpadding="8" style="border-collapse:collapse; width:100%; border-color:#30363d;">
    <thead><tr><th>Started</th><th>Repository</th><th>Issue</th><th>Outcome</th><th>Calls</th><th>Tokens</th></tr></thead>
    <tbody>{rows_html}</tbody>
  </table>
</body></html>"""
    output_path.write_text(page)
    return output_path

_EVENT_HISTORY: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
_SYSTEM_SPECS = get_system_info()
_LAST_LOG_MESSAGE = ""
_SHARED_ROUTER: Optional[ModelRouter] = None

# Global Human-in-the-loop Approval Registry
_PENDING_APPROVAL: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None
_APPROVAL_EVENT = threading.Event()
_APPROVAL_DECISION: bool = False


def get_shared_router() -> ModelRouter:
    global _SHARED_ROUTER
    if _SHARED_ROUTER is None:
        from config.settings import settings
        chains = settings.get_task_chains() if hasattr(settings, "get_task_chains") else getattr(settings, "TASK_CHAINS", {})
        _SHARED_ROUTER = ModelRouter(task_chains=chains)
    return _SHARED_ROUTER


def request_human_approval(title: str, description: str, diff: str = "", metadata: Dict[str, Any] = None) -> bool:
    """
    Blocks backend execution until approved via Web Dashboard UI or CLI fallback.
    """
    global _PENDING_APPROVAL, _APPROVAL_EVENT, _APPROVAL_DECISION
    _APPROVAL_EVENT.clear()
    _APPROVAL_DECISION = False

    _PENDING_APPROVAL = {
        "id": f"appr_{int(datetime.now().timestamp())}",
        "title": title,
        "description": description,
        "diff": diff,
        "metadata": metadata or {},
        "created_at": datetime.now().isoformat()
    }

    dashboard.emit("AWAITING_APPROVAL", _PENDING_APPROVAL)
    print(f"\n[HUMAN APPROVAL REQUIRED] {title}: {description}")
    print("Waiting for browser action at http://localhost:8000 ...")

    # Wait up to 10 minutes for web approval before falling back
    approved = _APPROVAL_EVENT.wait(timeout=600)
    decision = _APPROVAL_DECISION if approved else False
    _PENDING_APPROVAL = None

    dashboard.emit("APPROVAL_RESOLVED", {"approved": decision})
    return decision


class DashboardHTTPRequestHandler(http.server.SimpleHTTPRequestHandler):

    def translate_path(self, path: str) -> str:
        if path in ("/", "/index.html"):
            template_path = Path(__file__).parent / "templates" / "index.html"
            if template_path.exists():
                return str(template_path)
        return super().translate_path(path)

    def _set_json_headers(self, status: int = 200):
        self.send_response(status)
        self.send_header("Content-Type", "application/json")
        self.send_header("Access-Control-Allow-Origin", "*")
        self.send_header("Access-Control-Allow-Methods", "GET, POST, OPTIONS")
        self.send_header("Access-Control-Allow-Headers", "Content-Type, Authorization")
        self.end_headers()

    def do_OPTIONS(self):
        self._set_json_headers(200)

    def do_GET(self):
        if self.path == "/api/specs":
            self._set_json_headers(200)
            specs = dict(_SYSTEM_SPECS)
            specs["tier"] = HardwareSpecDetector.detect_tier()
            specs["ram_gb"] = HardwareSpecDetector.get_system_ram_gb()
            self.wfile.write(json.dumps(specs).encode("utf-8"))
            return

        if self.path == "/api/models":
            self._set_json_headers(200)
            router = get_shared_router()
            balance = router.get_account_balance()
            tier = router.hardware_tier
            ollama_online = router.local_client.is_available()

            data = {
                "tier": tier,
                "ram_gb": HardwareSpecDetector.get_system_ram_gb(),
                "openrouter_balance_usd": balance,
                "ollama_available": ollama_online,
                "mode": router.mode
            }
            self.wfile.write(json.dumps(data).encode("utf-8"))
            return

        if self.path == "/api/events":
            self._set_json_headers(200)
            self.wfile.write(json.dumps(_EVENT_HISTORY[-60:]).encode("utf-8"))
            return

        if self.path == "/api/approval":
            self._set_json_headers(200)
            self.wfile.write(json.dumps({"pending": _PENDING_APPROVAL}).encode("utf-8"))
            return

        return super().do_GET()

    def do_POST(self):
        global _APPROVAL_DECISION
        if self.path == "/api/ollama/pull":
            content_length = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length", 0))
            payload = json.loads(self.rfile.read(content_length).decode("utf-8"))
            model_name = payload.get("model", "").strip()

            if not model_name:
                self._set_json_headers(400)
                self.wfile.write(json.dumps({"error": "No model specified"}).encode("utf-8"))
                return

            def _pull_worker():
                dashboard.emit("STATE_CHANGE", {"state": "WORKING"})
                dashboard.emit("LOG_STREAM", {"message": f"Pulling model: {model_name} via Ollama..."})
                import subprocess
                try:
                    res = subprocess.run(["ollama", "pull", model_name], capture_output=True, text=True)
                    if res.returncode == 0:
                        dashboard.emit("LOG_STREAM", {"message": f"Successfully installed {model_name}"})
                        dashboard.emit("STATE_CHANGE", {"state": "SUCCESS"})
                    else:
                        dashboard.emit("LOG_STREAM", {"message": f"Ollama pull failed: {res.stderr[:80]}"})
                        dashboard.emit("STATE_CHANGE", {"state": "ERROR"})
                except Exception as ex:
                    dashboard.emit("LOG_STREAM", {"message": f"Ollama pull error: {str(ex)}"})
                    dashboard.emit("STATE_CHANGE", {"state": "ERROR"})

            threading.Thread(target=_pull_worker, daemon=True).start()
            self._set_json_headers(200)
            self.wfile.write(json.dumps({"status": "pull_started", "model": model_name}).encode("utf-8"))
            return

        if self.path == "/api/approval":
            content_length = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length", 0))
            payload = json.loads(self.rfile.read(content_length).decode("utf-8"))
            decision = bool(payload.get("approved", False))
            _APPROVAL_DECISION = decision
            _APPROVAL_EVENT.set()
            self._set_json_headers(200)
            self.wfile.write(json.dumps({"status": "resolved", "decision": decision}).encode("utf-8"))
            return

        if self.path == "/api/chat":
            content_length = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length", 0))
            payload = json.loads(self.rfile.read(content_length).decode("utf-8"))
            prompt = payload.get("prompt", "").strip()

            if not prompt:
                self._set_json_headers(400)
                self.wfile.write(json.dumps({"error": "Empty prompt"}).encode("utf-8"))
                return

            dashboard.emit("STATE_CHANGE", {"state": "THINKING"})
            dashboard.emit("AGENT_ACTIVE", {"name": "SUPERVISOR", "role": "Orchestrator", "status": "Deconstructing task"})
            dashboard.emit("LOG_STREAM", {"message": f"Supervisor planning: '{prompt[:45]}...'"})

            router = get_shared_router()
            messages = [
                {
                    "role": "system",
                    "content": (
                        "You are the Parent Supervisor of OSS-Agent, an adaptive, local-first engineering workstation. "
                        "Respond cleanly and concisely in structured markdown. For software build requests, present "
                        "explicit architectural specs and work breakdowns."
                    )
                },
                {"role": "user", "content": prompt}
            ]

            try:
                reply, model_used = router.complete_with_fallback("orchestration", messages)
                dashboard.emit("STATE_CHANGE", {"state": "SUCCESS"})
                dashboard.emit("LOG_STREAM", {"message": f"Resolved via [{model_used}]"})
                dashboard.emit("AGENT_INACTIVE", {"name": "SUPERVISOR"})

                self._set_json_headers(200)
                self.wfile.write(json.dumps({
                    "response": reply,
                    "model_used": model_used,
                    "status": "success"
                }).encode("utf-8"))
            except Exception as e:
                dashboard.emit("STATE_CHANGE", {"state": "ERROR"})
                dashboard.emit("LOG_STREAM", {"message": f"Pipeline failure: {str(e)}"})
                self._set_json_headers(500)
                self.wfile.write(json.dumps({"error": str(e)}).encode("utf-8"))
            return

        self._set_json_headers(404)
        self.wfile.write(json.dumps({"error": "Endpoint not found"}).encode("utf-8"))

    def log_message(self, format, *args):
        pass


class GlassBoxDashboardServer:
    def __init__(self, host: str = "0.0.0.0", port: int = 8000):
        self.host = host
        self.port = port
        self.server = None
        self.thread = None

    def start(self):
        def run_server():
            socketserver.TCPServer.allow_reuse_address = True
            with socketserver.TCPServer((self.host, self.port), DashboardHTTPRequestHandler) as httpd:
                self.server = httpd
                httpd.serve_forever()

        self.thread = threading.Thread(target=run_server, daemon=True)
        self.thread.start()
        print(f"[GlassBox Dashboard] Live workstation running at http://localhost:{self.port}")

    def emit(self, event_type: str, data: Dict[str, Any]):
        global _LAST_LOG_MESSAGE
        if event_type == "LOG_STREAM":
            msg = data.get("message", "").strip()
            if msg == _LAST_LOG_MESSAGE:
                return
            _LAST_LOG_MESSAGE = msg

        payload = {
            "type": event_type,
            "data": data,
            "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat()
        }
        _EVENT_HISTORY.append(payload)
        if len(_EVENT_HISTORY) > 100:
            _EVENT_HISTORY.pop(0)


dashboard = GlassBoxDashboardServer()

--- START FILE: tools/diff_explainer.py ---
import re
import shutil
import subprocess
from typing import List, Dict, Any
from tools.system_tier import CURRENT_TIER, HardwareTier

class DiffHunkExplainer:
    """
    Parses patch diffs into categorized hunks (Core Fix vs. Formatting/Churn)
    with structural diff tool support (difftastic) and low-tier regex fallback.
    """

    def __init__(self):
        self.has_difftastic = shutil.which("difft") is not None

    def get_structural_diff(self, file_a: str, file_b: str) -> str:
        """Uses difftastic on desktop/server, falls back to standard diff on Termux."""
        if self.has_difftastic and CURRENT_TIER != HardwareTier.TIER_LOW:
            res = subprocess.run(["difft", "--color", "never", file_a, file_b], capture_output=True, text=True)
            if res.returncode == 0 and res.stdout:
                return res.stdout

        res = subprocess.run(["git", "diff", "--no-index", file_a, file_b], capture_output=True, text=True)
        return res.stdout

    def parse_hunks(self, raw_diff: str) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
        """Parses unified git diff into individual hunk objects with classification."""
        if not raw_diff.strip():
            return []

        hunks = []
        current_file = None
        current_hunk = None

        for line in raw_diff.splitlines():
            if line.startswith("diff --git"):
                parts = line.split(" ")
                current_file = parts[-1].replace("b/", "")
                continue

            if line.startswith("@@"):
                if current_hunk:
                    self._classify_hunk(current_hunk)
                    hunks.append(current_hunk)
                current_hunk = {
                    "file": current_file or "unknown",
                    "header": line,
                    "added": [],
                    "removed": [],
                    "classification": "UNKNOWN",
                    "summary": ""
                }
                continue

            if current_hunk is not None:
                if line.startswith("+") and not line.startswith("+++"):
                    current_hunk["added"].append(line[1:])
                elif line.startswith("-") and not line.startswith("---"):
                    current_hunk["removed"].append(line[1:])

        if current_hunk:
            self._classify_hunk(current_hunk)
            hunks.append(current_hunk)

        return hunks

    def _classify_hunk(self, hunk: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        added_text = "".join(hunk["added"]).strip()
        removed_text = "".join(hunk["removed"]).strip()

        # Whitespace / formatting check
        if re.sub(r"\s+", "", added_text) == re.sub(r"\s+", "", removed_text):
            hunk["classification"] = "CHURN"
            hunk["summary"] = "Whitespace or code indentation reformatting."
            return

        # Import modifications
        if all(re.match(r"^(import|from)\s+", l.strip()) for l in hunk["added"] if l.strip()):
            hunk["classification"] = "DEPENDENCY"
            hunk["summary"] = "Module import modification."
            return

        # Functional Logic Patch
        hunk["classification"] = "CORE_FIX"
        hunk["summary"] = f"Alters logic in {hunk['file']} (+{len(hunk['added'])} / -{len(hunk['removed'])} lines)"

    def format_review_summary(self, raw_diff: str) -> str:
        """Generates an audit report suitable for human PR reviews."""
        hunks = self.parse_hunks(raw_diff)
        if not hunks:
            return "No structural code modifications detected."

        core_fixes = [h for h in hunks if h["classification"] == "CORE_FIX"]
        churn = [h for h in hunks if h["classification"] in ("CHURN", "DEPENDENCY")]

        lines = [
            f"### Structural Diff Audit ({len(hunks)} hunks analyzed)",
            f"**Core Functional Changes ({len(core_fixes)}):**"
        ]
        for c in core_fixes:
            lines.append(f"- `{c['file']}`: {c['summary']}")

        if churn:
            lines.append(f"\n**Secondary / Formatting Churn ({len(churn)}):**")
            for ch in churn:
                lines.append(f"- `{ch['file']}`: {ch['summary']}")

        return "\n".join(lines)


--- END FILE: tools/diff_explainer.py ---

--- START FILE: tools/environment_setup.py ---
import os
import sys
import shutil
import subprocess
from pathlib import Path
from typing import List, Optional


def _detect_platform() -> str:
    if "com.termux" in os.environ.get("PREFIX", "") or "TERMUX_VERSION" in os.environ:
        return "termux"
    if sys.platform == "darwin":
        return "mac"
    if sys.platform.startswith("linux"):
        return "linux"
    if sys.platform.startswith("win"):
        return "windows"
    return "unknown"


class EnvironmentBootstrapper:
    """
    On-demand dependency & environment bootstrapper.
    Invoked strictly after cloning a target repository to install only
    the compilers, package managers, and runtimes required by that repo.
    """

    def __init__(self):
        self.platform = _detect_platform()
        self.is_termux = self.platform == "termux"

    def _run_cmd(self, cmd: List[str]) -> bool:
        try:
            res = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=180)
            return res.returncode == 0
        except Exception:
            return False

    def bootstrap_environment(self) -> None:
        """Deprecated global startup installer. Kept as no-op for backward compatibility."""
        pass

    def bootstrap_for_repo(self, repo_dir: Optional[Path]) -> None:
        """Inspects target repo manifest files and installs missing toolchains just-in-time."""
        if not repo_dir or not Path(repo_dir).exists():
            return

        repo_path = Path(repo_dir)
        has = lambda name: (repo_path / name).exists()

        print(f"\n[EnvironmentBootstrapper] Checking toolchain requirements for {repo_path.name}...")

        # 1. Python tooling (Poetry / Flit / Pytest)
        is_poetry_project = has("poetry.lock")
        if not is_poetry_project and has("pyproject.toml"):
            try:
                content = (repo_path / "pyproject.toml").read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="ignore")
                is_poetry_project = "[tool.poetry]" in content
            except Exception:
                is_poetry_project = False

        if is_poetry_project:
            if not shutil.which("poetry"):
                print("[Auto-Installer] Repository requires 'poetry'. Installing...")
                cmd = ["pip", "install", "poetry", "--break-system-packages"] if self.is_termux else [sys.executable, "-m", "pip", "install", "poetry"]
                self._run_cmd(cmd)

        if has("requirements.txt") or has("setup.py") or has("pyproject.toml"):
            import importlib.util
            if importlib.util.find_spec("pytest") is None and not shutil.which("pytest"):
                print("[Auto-Installer] Test suite requires 'pytest'. Installing...")
                cmd = ["pip", "install", "pytest", "--break-system-packages"] if self.is_termux else [sys.executable, "-m", "pip", "install", "pytest"]
                self._run_cmd(cmd)

        # 2. Node.js (pnpm / yarn)
        if has("package.json"):
            if has("pnpm-lock.yaml") and not shutil.which("pnpm"):
                print("[Auto-Installer] Repository requires 'pnpm'. Installing...")
                self._run_cmd(["npm", "install", "-g", "pnpm"])
            elif has("yarn.lock") and not shutil.which("yarn"):
                print("[Auto-Installer] Repository requires 'yarn'. Installing...")
                self._run_cmd(["npm", "install", "-g", "yarn"])

        # 3. Rust / Cargo
        if has("Cargo.toml") and not shutil.which("cargo"):
            print("[Auto-Installer] Rust manifest detected. Installing rust toolchain...")
            if self.is_termux:
                self._run_cmd(["pkg", "install", "rust", "-y"])

        # 4. C / C++ (Meson / Ninja / CMake)
        if has("meson.build") and not shutil.which("meson"):
            print("[Auto-Installer] Meson build file detected. Installing build tools...")
            if self.is_termux:
                self._run_cmd(["pkg", "install", "ninja", "-y"])
                self._run_cmd(["pip", "install", "meson", "--break-system-packages"])
            else:
                self._run_cmd([sys.executable, "-m", "pip", "install", "meson", "ninja"])

        if has("CMakeLists.txt") and not shutil.which("cmake"):
            print("[Auto-Installer] CMakeLists detected. Installing cmake...")
            if self.is_termux:
                self._run_cmd(["pkg", "install", "cmake", "-y"])

        print("[EnvironmentBootstrapper] Pre-flight toolchain check complete.")

--- END FILE: tools/environment_setup.py ---

--- START FILE: tools/filesystem.py ---
import os
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, List, Optional


class SafeFileSystem:
    def __init__(self, workspace_root: Path, sentinel: Optional[Any] = None):
        self.workspace_root = workspace_root.resolve()
        if sentinel is False:
            self.sentinel = None
        elif sentinel is not None:
            self.sentinel = sentinel
        else:
            try:
                from agent.security.sentinel import SentinelMesh
                self.sentinel = SentinelMesh(strict_mode=True)
            except Exception:
                self.sentinel = None

    def _validate_path(self, relative_or_absolute_path: str) -> Path:
        target_path = (self.workspace_root / relative_or_absolute_path).resolve()
        if not str(target_path).startswith(str(self.workspace_root)):
            raise PermissionError(f"Access denied: Path '{target_path}' escapes workspace directory.")
        return target_path

    def read_file(self, path: str) -> str:
        safe_path = self._validate_path(path)
        if not safe_path.is_file():
            raise FileNotFoundError(f"File not found: {path}")
        with open(safe_path, "r", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as f:
            return f.read()

    def write_file(self, path: str, content: str) -> None:
        safe_path = self._validate_path(path)
        if self.sentinel:
            self.sentinel.validate_file_write(str(safe_path), content)
        safe_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        with open(safe_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(content)

    def list_files(self, relative_dir: str = ".") -> List[str]:
        safe_path = self._validate_path(relative_dir)
        results = []
        for root, _, files in os.walk(safe_path):
            for file in files:
                full = Path(root) / file
                rel = full.relative_to(self.workspace_root)
                results.append(str(rel))
        return results

--- END FILE: tools/filesystem.py ---

--- START FILE: tools/git.py ---
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Tuple
from tools.shell import SafeShell


class GitTools:
    def __init__(self, repo_dir: Path):
        self.shell = SafeShell(repo_dir)

    def checkout_branch(self, branch_name: str) -> Tuple[int, str, str]:
        return self.shell.execute(f"git checkout -b {branch_name}", auto_approve_override=True)

    def create_branch(self, branch_name: str) -> Tuple[int, str, str]:
        return self.checkout_branch(branch_name)

    def diff(self) -> str:
        return self.get_diff()

    def get_diff(self) -> str:
        code, stdout, _ = self.shell.execute("git diff", auto_approve_override=True)
        return stdout if code == 0 else ""

    def get_history(self, max_commits: int = 5) -> str:
        code, stdout, _ = self.shell.execute(f"git log -n {max_commits} --oneline", auto_approve_override=True)
        return stdout if code == 0 else ""

    def commit_all(self, message: str) -> Tuple[int, str, str]:
        self.shell.execute("git add -A", auto_approve_override=True)
        escaped_msg = message.replace('"', '\\"')
        return self.shell.execute(f'git commit -m "{escaped_msg}"')

    def push(self, remote: str, branch: str) -> Tuple[int, str, str]:
        return self.shell.execute(f"git push {remote} {branch}")

--- END FILE: tools/git.py ---

--- START FILE: tools/github.py ---
from typing import List, Dict, Any, Optional
from github.client import GitHubClient
from github.forks import ForkManager as _ForkManager
from github.issues import IssueManager as _IssueManager
from github.pull_requests import PullRequestManager as _PullRequestManager
from github.repositories import RepositoryManager as _RepositoryManager
from models.schemas import RepositoryInfo, IssueInfo


class ForkManager(_ForkManager):
    def __init__(self, client: Optional[GitHubClient] = None):
        super().__init__(client or GitHubClient())


class PullRequestManager(_PullRequestManager):
    def __init__(self, client: Optional[GitHubClient] = None):
        super().__init__(client or GitHubClient())


class RepositoryManager(_RepositoryManager):
    def __init__(self, client: Optional[GitHubClient] = None):
        super().__init__(client or GitHubClient())


class IssueSelector(_IssueManager):
    """
    Convenience wrapper around the real github.issues.IssueManager,
    adding repo-lookup and raw-issue-fetch helpers. Subclasses the real,
    verified package rather than reimplementing it, so this stays in
    sync with any future changes to github/issues.py.
    """

    def __init__(self, client: Optional[GitHubClient] = None):
        self.client = client or GitHubClient()
        super().__init__(self.client)

    def _dict_to_repo_info(self, data: Dict[str, Any]) -> RepositoryInfo:
        owner_name = data.get("owner", {}).get("login", "") if isinstance(data.get("owner"), dict) else ""
        return RepositoryInfo(
            owner=owner_name,
            name=data.get("name", ""),
            full_name=data.get("full_name", ""),
            clone_url=data.get("clone_url", ""),
            ssh_url=data.get("ssh_url", ""),
            default_branch=data.get("default_branch", "main"),
            description=data.get("description") or "",
            stargazers_count=data.get("stargazers_count", 0),
            language=data.get("language") or "",
        )

    def get_repo_info(self, repo_full_name: str) -> RepositoryInfo:
        data = self.client.request("GET", f"/repos/{repo_full_name}")
        return self._dict_to_repo_info(data)

    def find_best_repo(self, query: Optional[str] = None) -> Optional[RepositoryInfo]:
        search_q = query or "language:python stars:>500"
        endpoint = "/search/repositories"
        params = {"q": search_q, "sort": "stars", "order": "desc", "per_page": 5}
        res = self.client.request("GET", endpoint, params=params)
        items = res.get("items", [])
        if not items:
            return None
        return self._dict_to_repo_info(items[0])

    def get_issue(self, repo_full_name: str, issue_number: int) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """Returns the raw GitHub API issue payload — matches how
        agent/orchestrator.py already handles direct issue fetches
        elsewhere, converting to IssueInfo at the call site rather than
        here, so there's one consistent conversion path in the codebase."""
        return self.client.request("GET", f"/repos/{repo_full_name}/issues/{issue_number}")

    def get_issue_info(self, repo_full_name: str, issue_number: int) -> IssueInfo:
        """Same as get_issue(), but returns a converted IssueInfo object
        for callers that want the typed model directly."""
        data = self.get_issue(repo_full_name, issue_number)
        return IssueInfo(
            number=data["number"],
            title=data.get("title", ""),
            body=data.get("body") or "",
            html_url=data.get("html_url", ""),
            repository_full_name=repo_full_name,
            labels=[l.get("name", "") for l in data.get("labels", []) if isinstance(l, dict)],
        )

    def list_open_issues(self, repo_full_name: str) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
        return self.search_issues(repo_full_name, state="open", limit=10)


__all__ = ["ForkManager", "PullRequestManager", "IssueSelector", "RepositoryManager", "GitHubClient"]

--- END FILE: tools/github.py ---

--- START FILE: tools/multi_lang_ast.py ---
import ast
import re
from pathlib import Path
from typing import List, Dict, Optional

# Tree-sitter is optional. On Termux/Android, py-tree-sitter's compiled
# bindings frequently fail to build — this must never crash the import,
# only disable the tree-sitter path and fall back to regex.
try:
    import tree_sitter
    from tree_sitter_languages import get_language, get_parser
    TREE_SITTER_AVAILABLE = True
except Exception:
    TREE_SITTER_AVAILABLE = False


EXTENSION_LANGUAGE_MAP = {
    ".py": "python",
    ".js": "javascript",
    ".jsx": "javascript",
    ".ts": "typescript",
    ".tsx": "typescript",
    ".java": "java",
    ".c": "c",
    ".h": "c",
    ".cpp": "cpp",
    ".hpp": "cpp",
    ".cc": "cpp",
    ".go": "go",
    ".rs": "rust",
}

# Regex fallback patterns — used when tree-sitter is unavailable, or for
# any language tree-sitter doesn't have a grammar loaded for. Deliberately
# simple: this is a heuristic scanner, not a real parser, and is not
# expected to catch every edge case (generics, decorators split across
# lines, etc.) — it's a proportionate fallback, not a full parse.
REGEX_FUNCTION_PATTERNS = {
    "python": r'^\s*def\s+(\w+)\s*\(',
    "javascript": r'^\s*(?:export\s+)?(?:async\s+)?function\s+(\w+)\s*\(|'
                  r'^\s*(?:export\s+)?const\s+(\w+)\s*=\s*(?:async\s*)?\(',
    "typescript": r'^\s*(?:export\s+)?(?:async\s+)?function\s+(\w+)\s*\(|'
                  r'^\s*(?:export\s+)?const\s+(\w+)\s*=\s*(?:async\s*)?\(',
    "java": r'^\s*(?:public|private|protected)\s+(?:static\s+)?[\w<>\[\]]+\s+(\w+)\s*\(',
    "c": r'^\s*[\w\*]+\s+(\w+)\s*\([^;]*\)\s*\{',
    "cpp": r'^\s*[\w:\*<>~]+\s+(\w+)\s*\([^;]*\)\s*\{',
    "go": r'^\s*func\s+(?:\([^)]*\)\s*)?(\w+)\s*\(',
    "rust": r'^\s*(?:pub\s+)?fn\s+(\w+)\s*\(',
}


def detect_language(file_path: Path) -> Optional[str]:
    """Returns the detected language for a file, or None if unrecognized."""
    return EXTENSION_LANGUAGE_MAP.get(file_path.suffix.lower())


def _has_nearby_doc_comment(lines: List[str], func_line_index: int) -> bool:
    """
    Heuristic check: does a comment/docstring-like line appear immediately
    before this function definition? Checks up to 3 lines back for common
    comment markers such as //, #, /*, *, or triple-quote style strings.
    """
    comment_markers = ("//", "#", "/*", "*", '"""', "'''")
    for offset in range(1, 4):
        idx = func_line_index - offset
        if idx < 0:
            break
        stripped = lines[idx].strip()
        if not stripped:
            continue
        if stripped.startswith(comment_markers):
            return True
        break  # hit a non-comment, non-blank line — stop looking further back
    return False


def _extract_with_tree_sitter(content: str, language: str) -> List[Dict]:
    """
    Extracts function definitions using tree-sitter, when available and
    a grammar is loaded for this language. Returns a list of
    {name, line, has_doc} dicts. Falls back silently (returns []) on any
    parse error — caller is responsible for falling back to regex.
    """
    try:
        parser = get_parser(language)
        tree = parser.parse(bytes(content, "utf8"))
    except Exception:
        return []

    results = []
    lines = content.splitlines()

    # Node type names vary per grammar; check the common ones across
    # supported languages rather than a single universal type.
    function_node_types = {
        "function_definition", "function_declaration", "method_declaration",
        "function_item", "method_definition", "arrow_function",
    }

    def walk(node):
        if node.type in function_node_types:
            name = None
            for child in node.children:
                if child.type in ("identifier", "property_identifier", "field_identifier"):
                    name = content[child.start_byte:child.end_byte]
                    break
            line_no = node.start_point[0]
            has_doc = _has_nearby_doc_comment(lines, line_no)
            results.append({"name": name or "<anonymous>", "line": line_no + 1, "has_doc": has_doc})
        for child in node.children:
            walk(child)

    try:
        walk(tree.root_node)
    except Exception:
        return []

    return results


def _extract_with_regex(content: str, language: str) -> List[Dict]:
    """
    Extracts function definitions using a simple line-by-line regex scan.
    Used when tree-sitter is unavailable, or as a fallback for any
    language/file tree-sitter fails on. Heuristic, not exhaustive.
    """
    pattern = REGEX_FUNCTION_PATTERNS.get(language)
    if not pattern:
        return []

    compiled = re.compile(pattern)
    lines = content.splitlines()
    results = []

    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
        match = compiled.match(line)
        if match:
            name = next((g for g in match.groups() if g), "<anonymous>")
            has_doc = _has_nearby_doc_comment(lines, i)
            results.append({"name": name, "line": i + 1, "has_doc": has_doc})

    return results


def extract_functions(file_path: Path, content: str) -> List[Dict]:
    """
    Main entry point: extracts function definitions from a source file,
    each as {name, line, has_doc}. Tries tree-sitter first if available
    and the language is supported by it; falls back to regex on any
    failure or if tree-sitter isn't installed. Python files continue to
    use the stdlib ast module directly (see researcher.py), since that
    path is already exact and free — this module handles everything else.
    """
    language = detect_language(file_path)
    if not language:
        return []

    if TREE_SITTER_AVAILABLE:
        results = _extract_with_tree_sitter(content, language)
        if results:
            return results
        # Fall through to regex if tree-sitter found nothing (e.g. grammar
        # not loaded for this language, or the file failed to parse).

    return _extract_with_regex(content, language)

--- END FILE: tools/multi_lang_ast.py ---

--- START FILE: tools/pull_adapter.py ---
import argparse
import os
import sys
from pathlib import Path

# Ensure repo root is on sys.path regardless of execution working directory
REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
if str(REPO_ROOT) not in sys.path:
    sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO_ROOT))

from agent.training.adapter_registry import adapter_registry


def pull_adapter_from_hf(
    repo_id: str,
    role: str,
    token: str = None,
    base_model: str = "meta-llama/Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct",
) -> Path:
    """Downloads adapter repository from Hugging Face Hub into local .adapters/."""
    try:
        from huggingface_hub import snapshot_download
    except ImportError:
        print("[Error] huggingface_hub is required. Install via: pip install huggingface_hub")
        sys.exit(1)

    hf_token = token or os.environ.get("HF_TOKEN")
    dest_dir = Path(f".adapters/{role}/final_adapter").resolve()
    dest_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    print(f"[PullAdapter] Downloading '{repo_id}' -> {dest_dir}...")
    snapshot_download(
        repo_id=repo_id,
        local_dir=str(dest_dir),
        token=hf_token,
        ignore_patterns=["*.msgpack", "*.h5", "*.ot"],
    )

    # Register in central adapters_manifest.json
    record = adapter_registry.register_adapter(
        role=role,
        adapter_path=str(dest_dir),
        base_model=base_model,
        benchmark_score=1.0,
        training_samples=0,
        metadata={"source": "huggingface", "repo_id": repo_id},
    )

    print(f"[PullAdapter] Registered adapter for role '{role}' in manifest:")
    print(f"  • Path: {record['adapter_path']}")
    print(f"  • Base Model: {record['base_model']}")
    return dest_dir


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Pull trained LoRA adapter weights from Hugging Face.")
    parser.add_argument("--repo-id", required=True, help="HF adapter repository (e.g. username/oss-agent-mason-adapter)")
    parser.add_argument("--role", required=True, help="Agent role name (e.g. mason_backend_mason, reviewer, tester)")
    parser.add_argument("--token", help="Hugging Face read token (defaults to HF_TOKEN environment variable)")
    parser.add_argument("--base-model", default="meta-llama/Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct", help="Base model identifier")

    args = parser.parse_args()
    pull_adapter_from_hf(
        repo_id=args.repo_id,
        role=args.role,
        token=args.token,
        base_model=args.base_model,
    )


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

--- END FILE: tools/pull_adapter.py ---

--- START FILE: tools/repo_guard.py ---
import importlib.util
import os
import re
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, List, Optional
from tools.dashboard import dashboard


@dataclass
class MissingDependency:
    component: str          # e.g. "pytest module"
    reason: str             # human-readable explanation
    install_command: str    # exact copy-pasteable fix


@dataclass
class SecurityFinding:
    severity: str           # "HIGH", "CRITICAL", "MEDIUM"
    file_path: str
    line_number: int
    rule: str
    snippet: str


@dataclass
class GuardResult:
    ok: bool
    repo_name: str
    missing: List[MissingDependency] = field(default_factory=list)
    security_findings: List[SecurityFinding] = field(default_factory=list)


def _detect_platform() -> str:
    if "com.termux" in os.environ.get("PREFIX", ""):
        return "termux"
    if sys.platform == "darwin":
        return "mac"
    if sys.platform.startswith("linux"):
        return "linux"
    if sys.platform.startswith("win"):
        return "windows"
    return "unknown"


def _install_cmd(binary: str, platform: str, pip_fallback: Optional[str] = None) -> str:
    commands = {
        "termux": {
            "npm": "pkg install nodejs -y",
            "node": "pkg install nodejs -y",
            "cargo": "pkg install rust -y",
            "make": "pkg install make -y",
            "tox": "pip install tox --break-system-packages",
        },
        "mac": {
            "npm": "brew install node",
            "node": "brew install node",
            "cargo": "brew install rust",
            "make": "xcode-select --install",
            "tox": "pip install tox",
        },
        "linux": {
            "npm": "sudo apt install nodejs npm -y",
            "node": "sudo apt install nodejs -y",
            "cargo": "sudo apt install cargo -y",
            "make": "sudo apt install build-essential -y",
            "tox": "pip install tox",
        },
        "windows": {
            "npm": "winget install OpenJS.NodeJS",
            "node": "winget install OpenJS.NodeJS",
            "cargo": "winget install Rustlang.Rust.MSVC",
            "make": "choco install make",
            "tox": "pip install tox",
        },
    }
    platform_map = commands.get(platform, {})
    if binary in platform_map:
        return platform_map[binary]
    if pip_fallback:
        return pip_fallback
    return f"Install '{binary}' manually — no known command for this platform."


class SecurityAgent:
    """
    Audits codebase changes for secret exposure, credential leakage,
    eval execution, and injection vulnerabilities.
    """

    SECRET_PATTERNS = [
        ("CRITICAL", "OpenAI / OpenRouter API Key", re.compile(r"sk-[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{24,}")),
        ("CRITICAL", "GitHub Personal Access Token", re.compile(r"ghp_[a-zA-Z0-9]{36}")),
        ("CRITICAL", "Generic Secret Token", re.compile(r"""(?:api_key|token|secret|password)\s*[:=]\s*['"][a-zA-Z0-9_\-]{16,}['"]""", re.IGNORECASE)),
        ("HIGH", "Private Key Header", re.compile(r"-----BEGIN (?:RSA |EC |OPENSSH )?PRIVATE KEY-----")),
        ("HIGH", "Insecure Dynamic Execution", re.compile(r"""\b(?:eval|exec)\s*\((?![^)]*(?:True|False|None))\s*[^)]+\)""")),
        ("MEDIUM", "Shell Injection Risk", re.compile(r"""os\.system\s*\(\s*f['"]|subprocess\.(?:Popen|call|run)\s*\([^)]*shell\s*=\s*True""")),
    ]

    def scan_path(self, target_dir: Path) -> List[SecurityFinding]:
        findings: List[SecurityFinding] = []
        skip_dirs = {".git", "node_modules", "venv", "__pycache__", ".venv"}

        for root, dirs, files in os.walk(target_dir):
            dirs[:] = [d for d in dirs if d not in skip_dirs]
            for file in files:
                file_path = Path(root) / file
                if file_path.suffix in [".py", ".js", ".jsx", ".ts", ".tsx", ".env", ".json", ".yml", ".yaml"]:
                    try:
                        content = file_path.read_text(errors="ignore")
                        lines = content.splitlines()
                        for i, line in enumerate(lines, 1):
                            for sev, rule, pattern in self.SECRET_PATTERNS:
                                if pattern.search(line):
                                    findings.append(SecurityFinding(
                                        severity=sev,
                                        file_path=str(file_path.relative_to(target_dir)),
                                        line_number=i,
                                        rule=rule,
                                        snippet=line.strip()[:80],
                                    ))
                    except Exception:
                        continue
        return findings


class RepoGuard:
    """
    Pre-flight inspector and active security auditor.
    Validates environment binaries, package declarations, and code security.
    """

    def __init__(self):
        self.platform = _detect_platform()
        self.security_agent = SecurityAgent()

    def inspect_workspace(self, repo_path: Path) -> GuardResult:
        repo_name = repo_path.name
        missing: List[MissingDependency] = []

        has = lambda fname: (repo_path / fname).exists()

        # Python projects
        if has("pyproject.toml") or has("requirements.txt") or has("setup.py") or has("Pipfile") or has("pytest.ini"):
            if importlib.util.find_spec("pytest") is None:
                missing.append(MissingDependency(
                    component="pytest module",
                    reason="A Python test/build config file was found, but pytest is not installed for this interpreter.",
                    install_command="pip install pytest --break-system-packages"
                        if self.platform == "termux" else "pip install pytest",
                ))

            if has("requirements.txt"):
                unmet = self._check_requirements_installed(repo_path / "requirements.txt")
                if unmet:
                    install_cmd = "pip install -r requirements.txt --break-system-packages" \
                        if self.platform == "termux" else "pip install -r requirements.txt"
                    missing.append(MissingDependency(
                        component="requirements.txt packages",
                        reason=f"{len(unmet)} package(s) declared but not installed: {', '.join(unmet[:5])}"
                               + (" ..." if len(unmet) > 5 else ""),
                        install_command=f"cd {repo_path} && {install_cmd}",
                    ))

        # tox
        if has("tox.ini") and importlib.util.find_spec("tox") is None:
            missing.append(MissingDependency(
                component="tox",
                reason="tox.ini found but the 'tox' package is not installed.",
                install_command=_install_cmd("tox", self.platform, pip_fallback="pip install tox"),
            ))

        # Node projects
        if has("package.json"):
            from tools.tests import detect_node_package_manager, resolve_package_manager_command
            manager = detect_node_package_manager(repo_path)
            install_cmd = resolve_package_manager_command(repo_path)

            if install_cmd is None:
                missing.append(MissingDependency(
                    component=manager or "npm",
                    reason=f"package.json found (detected package manager: {manager}) but no execution binary found.",
                    install_command=_install_cmd(manager or "npm", self.platform),
                ))
            elif not (repo_path / "node_modules").exists():
                missing.append(MissingDependency(
                    component="node_modules",
                    reason=f"package.json found (detected package manager: {manager}) but dependencies not installed.",
                    install_command=f"cd {repo_path} && {install_cmd}",
                ))

        # Rust projects
        if has("Cargo.toml") and not shutil.which("cargo"):
            missing.append(MissingDependency(
                component="cargo",
                reason="Cargo.toml found but the Rust toolchain (cargo) is not installed on this host.",
                install_command=_install_cmd("cargo", self.platform),
            ))

        # C/C++ projects
        if has("CMakeLists.txt") and not shutil.which("cmake"):
            missing.append(MissingDependency(
                component="cmake",
                reason="CMakeLists.txt found but cmake is not installed on this host.",
                install_command={"termux": "pkg install cmake -y", "mac": "brew install cmake",
                                  "linux": "sudo apt install cmake -y",
                                  "windows": "winget install Kitware.CMake"}.get(self.platform, "Install cmake manually"),
            ))
        if has("meson.build") and not shutil.which("meson"):
            missing.append(MissingDependency(
                component="meson",
                reason="meson.build found but meson is not installed on this host.",
                install_command={"termux": "pip install meson ninja --break-system-packages",
                                  "mac": "brew install meson ninja",
                                  "linux": "sudo apt install meson ninja-build -y",
                                  "windows": "pip install meson ninja"}.get(self.platform, "pip install meson ninja"),
            ))

        # Makefile
        if has("Makefile") and not shutil.which("make"):
            missing.append(MissingDependency(
                component="make",
                reason="Makefile found but 'make' is not available on this host.",
                install_command=_install_cmd("make", self.platform),
            ))

        # Security check run
        security_findings = self.security_agent.scan_path(repo_path)
        if security_findings:
            dashboard.emit("LOG_STREAM", {"message": f"Security Guard flagged {len(security_findings)} warning(s)."})

        is_ok = len(missing) == 0 and not any(f.severity == "CRITICAL" for f in security_findings)
        return GuardResult(
            ok=is_ok,
            repo_name=repo_name,
            missing=missing,
            security_findings=security_findings
        )

    def _check_requirements_installed(self, requirements_path: Path) -> List[str]:
        unmet = []
        try:
            lines = requirements_path.read_text(errors="ignore").splitlines()
        except Exception:
            return unmet

        for line in lines:
            line = line.strip()
            if not line or line.startswith("#") or line.startswith("-"):
                continue
            pkg_name = line.split("==")[0].split(">=")[0].split("<=")[0].split("[")[0].strip()
            if not pkg_name:
                continue
            normalized = pkg_name.replace("-", "_")
            if importlib.util.find_spec(normalized) is None:
                result = subprocess.run(
                    [sys.executable, "-m", "pip", "show", pkg_name],
                    capture_output=True, text=True,
                )
                if result.returncode != 0:
                    unmet.append(pkg_name)
        return unmet


def format_guard_report(result: GuardResult) -> str:
    lines = [
        "=" * 60,
        "[SECURITY & ENVIRONMENT GUARD REPORT]",
        "=" * 60,
        f"Repository: {result.repo_name}",
        "",
    ]
    if result.missing:
        lines.append("--- MISSING HOST DEPENDENCIES ---")
        for i, dep in enumerate(result.missing, 1):
            lines.append(f"#{i}: {dep.component}")
            lines.append(f"  Reason: {dep.reason}")
            lines.append(f"  Fix:    {dep.install_command}")
            lines.append("")

    if result.security_findings:
        lines.append("--- SECURITY FINDINGS ---")
        for f in result.security_findings:
            lines.append(f"[{f.severity}] {f.rule} in {f.file_path}:{f.line_number}")
            lines.append(f"  Code: {f.snippet}")
            lines.append("")

    lines.append("=" * 60)
    return "\n".join(lines)

--- START FILE: tools/repo_map.py ---
import ast
import os
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Set

IGNORED_DIRS: Set[str] = {
    ".git",
    "__pycache__",
    ".venv",
    "venv",
    "env",
    ".experience",
    ".adapters",
    "node_modules",
    ".pytest_cache",
    ".mypy_cache",
    "build",
    "dist",
    "eggs",
    ".tox",
}


class SymbolExtractor(ast.NodeVisitor):
    """Walks an AST and extracts function, class, and method signatures."""

    def __init__(self):
        self.entries: List[str] = []
        self._current_class: Optional[str] = None

    def visit_ClassDef(self, node: ast.ClassDef):
        bases = []
        for b in node.bases:
            try:
                bases.append(ast.unparse(b))
            except Exception:
                pass
        bases_str = f"({', '.join(bases)})" if bases else ""
        self.entries.append(f"  class {node.name}{bases_str}:")
        prev_class = self._current_class
        self._current_class = node.name
        self.generic_visit(node)
        self._current_class = prev_class

    def visit_FunctionDef(self, node: ast.FunctionDef):
        self._record_func(node, is_async=False)

    def visit_AsyncFunctionDef(self, node: ast.AsyncFunctionDef):
        self._record_func(node, is_async=True)

    def _record_func(self, node, is_async: bool = False):
        args = []
        for arg in node.args.args:
            arg_str = arg.arg
            if arg.annotation:
                try:
                    arg_str += f": {ast.unparse(arg.annotation)}"
                except Exception:
                    pass
            args.append(arg_str)

        if node.args.vararg:
            args.append(f"*{node.args.vararg.arg}")
        if node.args.kwarg:
            args.append(f"**{node.args.kwarg.arg}")

        ret_str = ""
        if node.returns:
            try:
                ret_str = f" -> {ast.unparse(node.returns)}"
            except Exception:
                pass

        prefix = "async def" if is_async else "def"
        sig = f"{prefix} {node.name}({', '.join(args)}){ret_str}: ..."

        if self._current_class:
            self.entries.append(f"    {sig}")
        else:
            self.entries.append(f"  {sig}")


class RepoMapGenerator:
    """
    Generates a token-compact symbol skeleton of the codebase.
    Enables 3B models to inspect cross-module classes, methods, and
    function signatures without exceeding small context windows.
    """

    def __init__(self, workspace_root: Optional[Path] = None):
        self.workspace_root = (workspace_root or Path.cwd()).resolve()

    def _should_skip_dir(self, dir_path: Path) -> bool:
        return dir_path.name in IGNORED_DIRS or dir_path.name.startswith(".")

    def parse_file_symbols(self, file_path: Path) -> List[str]:
        try:
            content = file_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="ignore")
            tree = ast.parse(content, filename=str(file_path))
        except (SyntaxError, UnicodeDecodeError):
            return []

        extractor = SymbolExtractor()
        extractor.visit(tree)
        return extractor.entries

    def generate_map(self, max_chars: int = 4000) -> str:
        """
        Scans all Python files in the workspace and produces a compact
        AST skeleton string capped at max_chars (~1000 tokens).
        """
        lines = ["[CODEBASE SKELETON MAP]"]
        total_chars = 0

        for root, dirs, files in os.walk(self.workspace_root):
            # Prune ignored directories in-place
            dirs[:] = [d for d in dirs if not self._should_skip_dir(Path(root) / d)]

            rel_root = Path(root).relative_to(self.workspace_root)
            py_files = sorted([f for f in files if f.endswith(".py")])

            for py_file in py_files:
                file_path = Path(root) / py_file
                rel_path = (rel_root / py_file) if str(rel_root) != "." else Path(py_file)
                symbols = self.parse_file_symbols(file_path)

                if not symbols:
                    continue

                file_header = f"\n{rel_path}:"
                lines.append(file_header)
                total_chars += len(file_header)

                for sym in symbols:
                    if total_chars + len(sym) + 1 > max_chars:
                        lines.append("  ... [truncated: budget reached]")
                        return "\n".join(lines)
                    lines.append(sym)
                    total_chars += len(sym) + 1

        return "\n".join(lines)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    generator = RepoMapGenerator()
    repo_map = generator.generate_map()
    print(repo_map)

--- START FILE: tools/repomap.py ---
import os
import ast
import re
import shutil
import subprocess
from pathlib import Path
from typing import List, Dict, Any, Optional
from tools.system_tier import CURRENT_TIER, HardwareTier

class MultiLanguageASTParser:
    """
    Tier-adaptive parser providing full AST parsing for Python,
    tree-sitter/ctags for high tiers, and robust grammar extractors for
    JS/TS, Go, Rust, C/C++, and Java on edge/Termux.
    """

    # Language regex grammar patterns for non-Python code
    GRAMMAR_RULES: Dict[str, List[re.Pattern]] = {
        "javascript": [
            re.compile(r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:default\s+)?(?:async\s+)?function\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_$]+)\s*\([^)]*\)"),
            re.compile(r"^(?:export\s+)?class\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_$]+)(?:\s+extends\s+[a-zA-Z0-9_$]+)?"),
            re.compile(r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:const|let|var)\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_$]+)\s*=\s*(?:async\s*)?\([^)]*\)\s*=>"),
        ],
        "typescript": [
            re.compile(r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:default\s+)?(?:async\s+)?function\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_$]+)\s*(?:<[^>]+>)?\s*\([^)]*\)"),
            re.compile(r"^(?:export\s+)?class\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_$]+)(?:\s+implements\s+[^{]+)?"),
            re.compile(r"^(?:export\s+)?interface\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_$]+)"),
            re.compile(r"^(?:export\s+)?type\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_$]+)\s*="),
        ],
        "go": [
            re.compile(r"^func\s+(?:\([^)]+\)\s+)?([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)\s*\([^)]*\)"),
            re.compile(r"^type\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)\s+struct"),
            re.compile(r"^type\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)\s+interface"),
        ],
        "rust": [
            re.compile(r"^(?:pub(?:\([^)]+\))?\s+)?(?:async\s+)?fn\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)"),
            re.compile(r"^(?:pub(?:\([^)]+\))?\s+)?struct\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)"),
            re.compile(r"^(?:pub(?:\([^)]+\))?\s+)?enum\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)"),
            re.compile(r"^impl(?:\s*<[^>]+>)?\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)"),
        ],
        "cpp": [
            re.compile(r"^(?:[a-zA-Z0-9_:<>]+\s+)+([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)\s*\([^;)]*\)\s*(?:const)?\s*(?:override)?\s*\{?"),
            re.compile(r"^(?:class|struct)\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)"),
        ],
        "java": [
            re.compile(r"^(?:public|protected|private)?\s*(?:static\s+)?(?:final\s+)?(?:[a-zA-Z0-9_<>\[\]]+\s+)+([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)\s*\([^)]*\)"),
            re.compile(r"^(?:public\s+)?(?:abstract\s+)?class\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)"),
            re.compile(r"^(?:public\s+)?interface\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)"),
        ],
    }

    EXTENSION_MAP = {
        ".py": "python",
        ".js": "javascript", ".mjs": "javascript", ".jsx": "javascript",
        ".ts": "typescript", ".tsx": "typescript",
        ".go": "go",
        ".rs": "rust",
        ".cpp": "cpp", ".cc": "cpp", ".cxx": "cpp", ".hpp": "cpp", ".h": "cpp",
        ".java": "java",
    }

    def parse_file(self, path: Path, limit: int = 15) -> List[str]:
        ext = path.suffix.lower()
        lang = self.EXTENSION_MAP.get(ext)
        if not lang:
            return []

        if lang == "python":
            return self._parse_python(path, limit)
        return self._parse_grammar(path, lang, limit)

    def _parse_python(self, path: Path, limit: int) -> List[str]:
        symbols = []
        try:
            with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8", errors="ignore") as f:
                content = f.read(65536)
            tree = ast.parse(content, filename=str(path))
            for node in tree.body:
                if len(symbols) >= limit:
                    break
                if isinstance(node, ast.ClassDef):
                    methods = [n.name for n in node.body if isinstance(n, ast.FunctionDef)]
                    meth_str = f"({', '.join(methods[:3])})" if methods else ""
                    symbols.append(f"class {node.name}{meth_str}")
                elif isinstance(node, ast.FunctionDef):
                    args = [a.arg for a in node.args.args[:3]]
                    symbols.append(f"def {node.name}({', '.join(args)})")
        except Exception:
            # Fallback to regex if file has Python syntax errors
            return self._parse_grammar(path, "python", limit)
        return symbols

    def _parse_grammar(self, path: Path, lang: str, limit: int) -> List[str]:
        patterns = self.GRAMMAR_RULES.get(lang, [])
        if not patterns and lang == "python":
            patterns = [re.compile(r"^(?:class|def)\s+([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)")]

        symbols = []
        try:
            with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8", errors="ignore") as f:
                for line in f:
                    if len(symbols) >= limit:
                        break
                    s_line = line.strip()
                    for pat in patterns:
                        match = pat.search(s_line)
                        if match:
                            clean_sig = s_line.split("{")[0].strip().rstrip(":")
                            symbols.append(clean_sig)
                            break
        except Exception:
            pass
        return symbols


class RepoMapGenerator:
    """
    Tier-Dynamic Repository Map Generator supporting all major languages.
    """

    IGNORE_DIRS = {
        ".git", "node_modules", "__pycache__", "venv", ".venv",
        "build", "dist", "target", ".eggs", ".pytest_cache"
    }

    def __init__(self, max_tokens: Optional[int] = None):
        if max_tokens is not None:
            self.max_tokens = max_tokens
        else:
            self.max_tokens = 8192 if CURRENT_TIER == HardwareTier.TIER_HIGH else (2048 if CURRENT_TIER == HardwareTier.TIER_MID else 1024)

        self.max_chars = self.max_tokens * 4
        self.parser = MultiLanguageASTParser()
        self.has_ctags = shutil.which("ctags") is not None

    def generate(self, repo_dir: Path) -> str:
        if not repo_dir or not repo_dir.exists():
            return ""

        # High tier: use ctags when available for whole-repo symbol tables
        if CURRENT_TIER == HardwareTier.TIER_HIGH and self.has_ctags:
            map_str = self._generate_ctags(repo_dir)
            if map_str:
                return map_str

        return self._generate_multi_lang_ast(repo_dir)

    def _generate_multi_lang_ast(self, repo_dir: Path) -> str:
        lines: List[str] = []

        for root, dirs, files in os.walk(repo_dir):
            dirs[:] = [d for d in dirs if d not in self.IGNORE_DIRS and not d.startswith(".")]

            for file in files:
                if len("".join(lines)) >= self.max_chars:
                    break

                file_path = Path(root) / file
                symbols = self.parser.parse_file(file_path)
                if symbols:
                    rel_path = file_path.relative_to(repo_dir)
                    lines.append(f"{rel_path}:")
                    lines.extend(f"  {s}" for s in symbols)

        joined = "\n".join(lines)
        if CURRENT_TIER != HardwareTier.TIER_HIGH and len(joined) > self.max_chars:
            return joined[:self.max_chars] + "\n... [RepoMap compressed for context budget]"
        return joined

    def _generate_ctags(self, repo_dir: Path) -> str:
        try:
            cmd = ["ctags", "-R", "--output-format=json", "--fields=+n", "-f", "-", str(repo_dir)]
            res = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=12)
            if res.returncode == 0 and res.stdout:
                import json
                out_lines = []
                for line in res.stdout.splitlines()[:250]:
                    tag = json.loads(line)
                    out_lines.append(f"{tag.get('path', '')}: {tag.get('kind', '')} {tag.get('name', '')}")
                return "\n".join(out_lines)
        except Exception:
            pass
        return ""

--- START FILE: tools/run_logger.py ---
import json
import uuid
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path
from models.schemas import RunLog, ModelCallLog


class RunLogger:
    """
    Writes one JSON file per run to logs/, plus appends a one-line
    summary to logs/history.jsonl for quick scanning across many runs.
    This is the foundation for later analysis: which models actually
    get used, cost trends, pass/fail rates over time.
    """
    def __init__(self, logs_dir: Path = None, dry_run: bool = False):
        self.logs_dir = logs_dir or (Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "logs")
        self.logs_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

        self.log = RunLog(
            run_id=str(uuid.uuid4())[:8],
            started_at=datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),
            dry_run=dry_run,
        )

    def set_repo_issue(self, repo_full_name: str, issue_number: int, issue_title: str):
        self.log.repository = repo_full_name
        self.log.issue_number = issue_number
        self.log.issue_title = issue_title

    def note(self, message: str):
        self.log.notes.append(message)

    def set_outcome(self, outcome: str):
        self.log.outcome = outcome

    def set_test_result(self, passed: bool, retries_used: int):
        self.log.test_pass = passed
        self.log.debug_retries_used = retries_used

    def set_review_result(self, approved: bool):
        self.log.review_approved = approved

    def attach_model_calls(self, calls: list[ModelCallLog]):
        self.log.model_calls = calls

    def estimated_cost_summary(self) -> dict:
        total_prompt = sum(c.prompt_tokens for c in self.log.model_calls)
        total_completion = sum(c.completion_tokens for c in self.log.model_calls)
        by_model = {}
        for c in self.log.model_calls:
            by_model.setdefault(c.model_used, {"prompt_tokens": 0, "completion_tokens": 0, "calls": 0})
            by_model[c.model_used]["prompt_tokens"] += c.prompt_tokens
            by_model[c.model_used]["completion_tokens"] += c.completion_tokens
            by_model[c.model_used]["calls"] += 1
        return {
            "total_prompt_tokens": total_prompt,
            "total_completion_tokens": total_completion,
            "by_model": by_model,
        }

    def finalize(self):
        self.log.finished_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()

        run_file = self.logs_dir / f"run-{self.log.run_id}.json"
        run_file.write_text(self.log.model_dump_json(indent=2))

        summary = {
            "run_id": self.log.run_id,
            "started_at": self.log.started_at,
            "finished_at": self.log.finished_at,
            "repository": self.log.repository,
            "issue_number": self.log.issue_number,
            "outcome": self.log.outcome,
            "dry_run": self.log.dry_run,
            "model_calls": len(self.log.model_calls),
            **self.estimated_cost_summary(),
        }
        history_file = self.logs_dir / "history.jsonl"
        with open(history_file, "a") as f:
            f.write(json.dumps(summary) + "\n")

        print(f"\n[RunLogger] Run log saved: {run_file}")
